   245	        // -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   246	        // LAMB ADDED-MASS k-FACTORS -- exact ellipsoid theory by deterministic quadrature.
   247	        // -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   248	
   249	        /// Added-mass coefficient k_i for translation along each semi-axis of an ellipsoid (a,b,c).
   250	        /// k_i = alpha_i / (2 - alpha_i), where alpha_i = a*b*c * Integral_0^inf dλ / ((axis_i^2 + λ) * Δ(λ)),
   251	        /// Δ = sqrt((a^2+λ)(b^2+λ)(c^2+λ)) (Lamb, Hydrodynamics §114). Sphere -> 0.5 each; slender body ->
   252	        /// transverse ->1, axial ->~0. Computed ONCE per segment (not in the hot loop). Deterministic:
   253	        /// fixed-step composite-Simpson quadrature in double, the substitution λ = L*t/(1-t) folding [0,inf)
   254	        /// onto t in [0,1). The exact identity alpha+beta+gamma = 2 is enforced by normalisation, which
   255	        /// removes the common quadrature bias and makes the sphere case bit-exact.
   256	        internal static void LambK(float a, float b, float c, out float kx, out float ky, out float kz)
   257	        {
   258	            double a2 = (double)a * a, b2 = (double)b * b, c2 = (double)c * c;
   259	            double abc = (double)a * b * c;
   260	            double L = a2; if (b2 > L) L = b2; if (c2 > L) L = c2;   // scale = max semi-axis^2
   261	            if (L <= 0.0) { kx = ky = kz = 0.5f; return; }          // degenerate guard -> sphere defaults
   262	
   263	            const int N = 2048;             // even number of Simpson intervals (fixed -> deterministic)
   264	            double sx = 0.0, sy = 0.0, sz = 0.0;
   265	            for (int i = 0; i <= N; i++)
   266	            {
   267	                double t = (double)i / N;
   268	                double om = 1.0 - t;
   269	                double gx, gy, gz;
   270	                if (om < 1e-9) { gx = 0.0; gy = 0.0; gz = 0.0; }    // integrand -> 0 like (1-t)^(1/2) at the tail
   271	                else
   272	                {
   273	                    double lam = L * t / om;
   274	                    double jac = L / (om * om);                      // dλ/dt
   275	                    double fa = a2 + lam, fb = b2 + lam, fc = c2 + lam;
   276	                    double baseTerm = jac / System.Math.Sqrt(fa * fb * fc);
   277	                    gx = baseTerm / fa; gy = baseTerm / fb; gz = baseTerm / fc;
   278	                }
   279	                double w = (i == 0 || i == N) ? 1.0 : ((i % 2 == 1) ? 4.0 : 2.0);
   280	                sx += w * gx; sy += w * gy; sz += w * gz;
   281	            }
   282	            double h = 1.0 / N;
   283	            double alpha = abc * sx * h / 3.0;
   284	            double beta = abc * sy * h / 3.0;
   285	            double gamma = abc * sz * h / 3.0;
   286	            double sum = alpha + beta + gamma;
   287	            if (sum > 1e-12) { double scl = 2.0 / sum; alpha *= scl; beta *= scl; gamma *= scl; }   // enforce alpha+beta+gamma=2
   288	            kx = (float)(alpha / (2.0 - alpha));
   289	            ky = (float)(beta / (2.0 - beta));
   290	            kz = (float)(gamma / (2.0 - gamma));
   320	        //     P_in    = m_t * U * ( V_t*W_t )                   (rate body works on the fluid)
   321	        //     P_wake  = 0.5 * m_t * U * W_t^2                   (irrecoverable shed wake KE)
   322	        //
   323	        // The thrust term ALGEBRAICALLY reduces (substitute W_t = V_t + U s, expand) to the classic
   324	        // Lighthill form, which is what ReactiveThrust evaluates:
   325	        //
   326	        //     T_react = 0.5 * m_t * ( V_t^2 - U^2 * s^2 )
   327	        //
   328	        // Consequences that EMERGE (no penalty terms anywhere):
   329	        //   * Traveling wave h = a sin(kx - wt): <T_react> = 1/4 m_t a^2 k^2 (c^2 - U^2) > 0 for U<c,
   330	        //     -> 0 as U->c (the famous EBT result; verified in the litmus).
   331	        //   * No lateral motion (V_t=0) on a straight tail (s=0) -> T_react = 0 (rigid body, L1).
   332	        //   * A standing/incoherent gait nets ~0 thrust at its operating speed (V_t^2 = U^2 s^2) yet
   333	        //     still sheds wake KE (W_t != 0) -> cost-of-transport -> infinity (L2). A tail-wag IS
   334	        //     propulsive (sculling); coherence shows up as EFFICIENCY (lower wake for equal thrust),
   335	        //     not as a thrust penalty.
   336	        //   * Energy closes EXACTLY, instantaneously: P_in == T_react*U + P_wake.
   337	
   338	        /// Crossflow shed at the trailing edge: W_t = V_t + U*s = dh/dt + U dh/dx.
   339	        public static float Crossflow(float Vt, float U, float s) => Vt + U * s;
   340	
   341	        /// Reactive (thrust) boundary term, Lighthill reduced form: T_react = 0.5 m_t (V_t^2 - U^2 s^2).
   342	        /// (Identically equal to the raw flux form m_t (V_t*W_t - 0.5 W_t^2); see ReactiveThrustFlux.)
   343	        public static float ReactiveThrust(float mt, float Vt, float U, float s)
   344	            => 0.5f * mt * (Vt * Vt - U * U * s * s);
   345	
   346	        /// The same reactive thrust written as the raw shed-momentum flux m_t (V_t*W_t - 0.5 W_t^2).
   347	        /// Provided so a test can confirm the two forms agree (the algebraic reduction holds numerically).
   348	        public static float ReactiveThrustFlux(float mt, float Vt, float U, float s)
   349	        {
   350	            float Wt = Crossflow(Vt, U, s);
   351	            return mt * (Vt * Wt - 0.5f * Wt * Wt);
   352	        }
   353	
   354	        /// Irrecoverable wake kinetic energy shed per unit time: P_wake = 0.5 m_t U W_t^2 (>= 0 for U>=0).
   355	        public static float WakePower(float mt, float U, float Wt) => 0.5f * mt * U * Wt * Wt;
   356	
   357	        /// Rate the body does work on the fluid: P_in = m_t U V_t W_t. Closes energy: P_in = T_react U + P_wake.
   358	        public static float InputPower(float mt, float U, float Vt, float Wt) => mt * U * Vt * Wt;
   359	
   360	        /// Transverse (lateral) added mass PER UNIT LENGTH at the tail: m_t = m_a_lateral / (2c).
   361	        /// The lateral crossflow acts along the tail's local x (width); 2c is the tail length (local +z).
   362	        internal static float TailMassPerLength(in Segment tail)
   363	            => tail.maX / Mathf.Max(2f * tail.c, 1e-4f);
   364	
   365	        // --- Circulatory (finite-wing foil) thrust: Garrick's OTHER half, for a caudal FIN (Surface tail) ---
   366	        // The reactive term above is the added-mass half (m_t). A thin, high-aspect-ratio caudal fin ALSO
   367	        // carries bound-circulation (Kutta-Joukowski) lift, which the elongated-body reactive term omits. This
   368	        // supplies that second, physically-distinct half (additive, not a double-count). Lift uses the FORWARD
   369	        // throughflow U as the KJ freestream (so lift -> 0 at rest); the finite-wing slope aL and induced drag
   370	        // CDi = CL^2/(pi*e*AR) make ASPECT RATIO the efficiency lever; the fin is FEATHERED by the tail slope s
   371	        // so it holds a near-optimal angle of attack across the stroke. Pure + deterministic + allocation-free.
   372	        internal const float FinProfileCd = 0.02f;   // thin-foil profile/skin-friction drag coefficient
   373	        internal const float FinSpanEff   = 0.9f;    // Oswald span efficiency (~0.9 for near-elliptical loading)
   374	        internal const float FinStallAoA  = 0.35f;   // ~20 deg attached-flow AoA limit (dynamic-stall bound)
   375	
   376	        /// Foil-plane alignment: |n̂_fin · n̂_heave|. The plate is thin in local +Y, so its normal is
   377	        /// restRot·up; lateralUnit is the swim-plane lateral heave direction. 0 = edge-on (no circulatory
   378	        /// lift), 1 = broadside (full lift). Pure + deterministic. (Garrick 1936: circulatory lift comes from
   379	        /// the flow component NORMAL to the foil; only heave along n̂_fin creates angle of attack.)
   380	        public static float FinAlign(Quaternion restRot, Vector3 lateralUnit)
   381	            => Mathf.Abs(Vector3.Dot(restRot * Vector3.up, lateralUnit.normalized));
   382	
   383	        /// Reactive-channel orientation scale: the fin's added mass along the lateral heave is m_perp·align²
   384	        /// (flat-plate added-mass tensor contracted twice with the motion direction; Lamb 1932). This returns the
   385	        /// gated factor Lerp(1, align², finPlaneGain): 1 at gain 0 (byte-neutral), align² at gain 1 (0 edge-on, 1 broadside).
   386	        public static float FinReactiveScaleOf(float align, float finPlaneGain)
   387	            => Mathf.Lerp(1f, align * align, finPlaneGain);
   388	
   389	        /// Circulatory foil thrust [N] + its induced/profile wake loss pWake [W]. aL = finite-wing lift slope
   390	        /// (1/rad), AR = span/chord, S = planform area [m^2]; U = forward speed, Vt = lateral heave speed,
   391	        /// s = tail slope (feathering pitch = dh/dx). Energy closes EXACTLY: P_in = T*U + pWake (see companion).
   392	        public static float CirculatoryLiftThrust(float aL, float AR, float S, float U, float Vt, float s, out float pWake)
   393	        {
   394	            pWake = 0f;
   395	            float Ucl = Mathf.Max(0f, U);                        // no circulatory lift travelling backward
   396	            float Q2 = Ucl * Ucl + Vt * Vt;
   397	            if (Q2 < 1e-8f) return 0f;                           // truly still -> no throughflow -> no lift
   398	            float Q = Mathf.Sqrt(Q2);
   399	            float pitch = Mathf.Asin(Mathf.Clamp(s, -1f, 1f));   // fin geometric pitch from the backbone slope
   400	            float alpha = Mathf.Clamp(Mathf.Atan2(Vt, Ucl) - pitch, -FinStallAoA, FinStallAoA);   // feathered AoA
   401	            float CL  = aL * alpha;
   402	            float q   = 0.5f * RhoWater * Ucl * Ucl * S;         // KJ dynamic pressure on the FORWARD throughflow
   403	            float L   = q * CL;
   404	            float CDi = FinProfileCd + (CL * CL) / (Mathf.PI * FinSpanEff * Mathf.Max(AR, 1e-4f));
   405	            float D   = q * CDi;
   406	            float sinB = Vt / Q, cosB = Ucl / Q;
   407	            pWake = D * Q;                                       // shed downwash + profile loss (>= 0)
   408	            return L * sinB - D * cosB;                          // forward: lift tilt minus induced-drag retardation
   409	        }
   410	
   555	                _nThrust = Vector3.Cross(Vector3.up, fThrust).normalized;   // lateral horizontal unit
   556	                _mt = segs.Count > 0 ? TailMassPerLength(segs[tail]) : 0f;
   557	                _finPlaneGain = finPlaneGain;
   558	                // Circulatory foil channel (Garrick's circulatory half): ON when the trailing edge is a Surface
   559	                // fin. Read the fin's TRUE span/chord from the retained gene (the physics build collapsed the
   560	                // plate). Chord uses a small anti-zero guard, NOT MinZ -- MinZ would throttle the aspect ratio
   561	                // into the low-efficiency regime and defeat the point. Segment tails leave the channel OFF.
   562	                if (segs.Count > 0 && segs[tail].gene != null && segs[tail].gene.type == PartType.Surface)
   563	                {
   564	                    float span  = Mathf.Max(segs[tail].gene.size.y, MinY);
   565	                    float chord = Mathf.Max(segs[tail].gene.size.z, 0.03f);
   566	                    _finS  = span * chord;
   567	                    _finAR = span / chord;
   568	                    _finAL = 2f * Mathf.PI * _finAR / (_finAR + 2f);
   569	                    _finActive = true;
   570	                    _finAlign = FinAlign(segs[tail].restRot, _nThrust);   // n̂_heave == _nThrust (lateral horizontal unit)
   571	                }
   572	                // Reactive thrust reads the fin's added mass ALONG the lateral heave: m_perp·align² (gated). For a fin tail,
   573	                // _mt started from tail.maX (= m_perp at gain 0, byte-neutral); rebuild it from the retained finMaPerp so the
   574	                // projection is exact even after the tensor moved m_perp onto maY at gain>0.
   575	                if (segs.Count > 0 && segs[tail].finMaPerp > 0f)
   576	                    _mt = segs[tail].finMaPerp * FinReactiveScaleOf(_finAlign, finPlaneGain) / Mathf.Max(2f * segs[tail].c, 1e-4f);
   577	                _Cyaw = ComputeCyaw(segs, cd);                              // rest-pose yaw form-drag coeff (Part A/§11.1)
   578	                _maxTurnCmd = DeriveMaxTurnCmd(segs);                       // Task 7: AmpMax-safe turn-bias cap
   579	                _gainK = _maxTurnCmd / FullAuthorityErrRad;                 // Task 4: reconcile rad-error -> deg/level scale
   580	                _pos = new Vector3[segs.Count];
   581	                _prevPos = new Vector3[segs.Count];
   582	                _rot = new Quaternion[segs.Count];
   583	                _xCom = Vector3.zero; _vCom = v0; _t = 0f;
   584	                Pose(0f);
   585	                for (int i = 0; i < _pos.Length; i++) _prevPos[i] = _pos[i];   // zero initial flex velocity
   655	            }
   656	
   657	            public Vector3 Velocity => _vCom;
   658	
   659	            // Forward kinematics: pose every segment in the body frame at time `time`, applying each
   660	            // joint's gait angle about its local up. Parents precede children in DFS order, so a single
   661	            // forward pass suffices. (No GameObjects -- pure quaternion algebra.)
   662	            void Pose(float time)
   663	            {
   664	                float w = 2f * Mathf.PI * _swimFreq;
   665	                for (int j = 0; j < _segs.Count; j++)
   666	                {
   667	                    var sg = _segs[j];
   668	                    Vector3 pPos; Quaternion pRot;
   669	                    if (sg.parentIndex < 0) { pPos = Vector3.zero; pRot = Quaternion.identity; }
   670	                    else { pPos = _pos[sg.parentIndex]; pRot = _rot[sg.parentIndex]; }
   671	
   672	                    Quaternion localFlexed = sg.localRot;
   673	                    if (sg.hasJoint)
   674	                    {
   675	                        float theta = sg.ampDeg * Mathf.Sin(w * time + sg.phase);   // degrees
   676	                        // LIVE turning bias (spec §4.1): a progressive DC body bend, head->tail, that makes
   677	                        // the evolved gait asymmetric so the EBT force law produces a net yaw torque (Task 3).
   678	                        // GUARDED on `_turnCmd != 0f` so the one-shot measurement path -- where `_turnCmd` is
   679	                        // always the default 0 -- executes the ORIGINAL expression verbatim (no `+0f` add, hence
   680	                        // no -0.0f/+0.0f sign-of-zero drift) => Pose is BIT-IDENTICAL there. On the live path
   681	                        // (`_turnCmd != 0`) this adds `turnCmd*depth` degrees to each jointed segment.
   682	                        if (_turnCmd != 0f) theta += _turnCmd * sg.depth;
   683	                        localFlexed = sg.localRot * Quaternion.AngleAxis(theta, Vector3.up);
   684	                    }
   685	                    _rot[j] = pRot * localFlexed;
   686	                    _pos[j] = pPos + pRot * sg.localPos;
   687	                }
   688	            }
   689	
   690	            Vector3 ComWorld()
   691	            {
   692	                Vector3 c = Vector3.zero;
   693	                for (int j = 0; j < _segs.Count; j++) c += _segs[j].mass * _pos[j];
   694	                return _xCom + (_mBody > 1e-6f ? c / _mBody : Vector3.zero);
   695	            }
   696	
   697	            // The COM in world space. On the LIVE path `_xCom` IS the COM (COM-direct, §11.3), so ComNow
   698	            // returns it directly. On the one-shot path `_live` is false and this returns the ORIGINAL
   699	            // ComWorld() (where `_xCom` is the body origin) -> the measurement path is unchanged.
   700	            internal Vector3 ComNow() => _live ? _xCom : ComWorld();
   701	
   702	            internal Quaternion OrientationNow() => _orientation;
   703	
   704	            // Yaw angular velocity (rad/s) accessor -- the §11.1 guard test asserts |OmegaNow()| never reaches
   705	            // MaxOmega during a normal turn (drag-set, not clamp-set). Read-only view of _omegaY.
   706	            internal float OmegaNow() => _omegaY;
   707	
   708	            // Task 7 test accessor: the derived AmpMax-safe turn-bias cap for this body.
   709	            internal float MaxTurnCmd => _maxTurnCmd;
   710	            internal static float MaxOmegaBackstop => MaxOmega;   // [EBT] instrumentation: the §11.1 numerical yaw-rate cap
   711	
   712	            // Segment count (== reconstructed parts). Render/skin bone count.
   713	            internal int SegCount => _pos.Length;
   714	
   715	            // Fill the per-segment WORLD pose for the LIVE path (COM-direct), Unity-light (writes caller arrays).
   716	            // World pos = COM + orientation*(bodyFrameSegPos - bodyFrameCom); world rot = orientation*bodyFrameSegRot.
   717	            // Reads the current gait pose (set by the last Pose(_t) in StepLive). Render-only accessor.
   718	            internal void FillSegmentPoses(Vector3[] pos, Quaternion[] rot)
   719	            {
   720	                Vector3 comBF = Vector3.zero;
   721	                for (int j = 0; j < _segs.Count; j++) comBF += _segs[j].mass * _pos[j];
   722	                comBF = _mBody > 1e-6f ? comBF / _mBody : Vector3.zero;
   723	                for (int j = 0; j < _pos.Length; j++)
   724	                {
   725	                    pos[j] = _xCom + _orientation * (_pos[j] - comBF);
   726	                    rot[j] = _orientation * _rot[j];
   727	                }
   728	            }
   729	
   730	            // Tail TIP world point in the body frame (excludes _xCom): the tail CENTER plus the tail's OWN
   731	            // flexed +z axis times its half-length c. Unlike _pos[_tail] (the center), the tip MOVES with the
   732	            // tail's own wag: Pose folds a segment's own joint rotation into _rot[j] but NOT into _pos[j]
   733	            // (_pos[j] = pPos + pRot*localPos uses only the PARENT chain). Sampling U, V_t AND the slope s all
   734	            // at this single tip keeps Lighthill's T = 1/4 m_t a^2 k^2 (c^2 - U^2) self-consistent (the EBT
   735	            // derivation assumes trailing-EDGE/tip evaluation, not segment-center).
   736	            Vector3 TailTip()
   737	                => _segs.Count > 0 ? _pos[_tail] + _rot[_tail] * Vector3.forward * _segs[_tail].c : Vector3.zero;
   738	
   739	            // One fixed semi-implicit step. `measuring` gates impulse/work accumulation.
   740	            public void Step(bool measuring)
   741	            {
   742	                _t += Dt;
   743	                for (int i = 0; i < _pos.Length; i++) _prevPos[i] = _pos[i];
   744	                Vector3 prevTip = TailTip();                             // tail TIP at the PREVIOUS pose (pre-Pose arrays)
   745	                Pose(_t);
   746	
   747	                // --- trailing-edge reactive thrust (the only thrust term), sampled at the tail TIP ---
   748	                Vector3 tipNow = TailTip();                             // tail TIP at the new pose
   749	                Vector3 uTail = _vCom + (tipNow - prevTip) / Dt;        // tail-TIP world velocity (captures the tail's own wag)
   750	                float U = Vector3.Dot(uTail, _fThrust);
   751	                float Vt = Vector3.Dot(uTail, _nThrust);
   752	                Vector3 tHat = _rot[_tail] * Vector3.forward;            // tail backbone (local +z) in world
   753	                float s = Vector3.Dot(tHat, _nThrust);
   754	                float tReact = ReactiveThrust(_mt, Vt, U, s);
   755	                float Wt = Crossflow(Vt, U, s);
   756	                float pWake = WakePower(_mt, Mathf.Max(0f, U), Wt);
   757	
   758	                // Circulatory foil channel (Garrick's other half; ONLY a Surface caudal fin). The reactive term
   759	                // above already carries the fin's ADDED-MASS half via its restored m_t (self-start); this adds the
   760	                // bound-circulation LIFT (efficiency at speed). Distinct physics -> additive, not a double-count.
   761	                float tFin = 0f, pFin = 0f;
   762	                if (_finActive)
   763	                {
   764	                    float vtEff = Vt * Mathf.Lerp(1f, _finAlign, _finPlaneGain);   // Garrick lift ∝ heave·normal; = Vt at gain 0
   765	                    tFin = CirculatoryLiftThrust(_finAL, _finAR, _finS, U, vtEff, s, out pFin);
   766	                }
   767	
   768	                // --- AXIAL-ONLY form drag over ALL segments (dissipative) + effective mass matrix ---
   769	                Vector3 fDrag = Vector3.zero;
   770	                float wDrag = 0f;
   771	                float m00 = _mBody * KgPerSimMass, m01 = 0f, m02 = 0f, m11 = _mBody * KgPerSimMass, m12 = 0f, m22 = _mBody * KgPerSimMass;   // structural inertia in SI kg (gene-mass * KgPerSimMass) -> matches the SI added-mass tensor below
   772	                float half = 0.5f * RhoWater * _cd;
   773	                for (int j = 0; j < _segs.Count; j++)
   774	                {
   775	                    var sg = _segs[j];
   776	                    Quaternion R = _rot[j];
   777	                    Vector3 uj = _vCom + (_pos[j] - _prevPos[j]) / Dt;
   778	                    Vector3 vloc = Quaternion.Inverse(R) * uj;            // segment velocity in local frame
   779	                    // Charge form drag ONLY on the streamwise local +z (frontal area areaZ) -- genuine
   780	                    // skin/form friction. The LATERAL broadside terms (areaX width, areaY height) are
   781	                    // deliberately NOT charged: a slender undulator's lateral resistance is REACTIVE
   782	                    // (added mass), already modeled by the EBT m_t / pWake / tReact reaction below;
   783	                    // charging a v^2 form drag on the same lateral tail-wag double-counts that momentum
   784	                    // and blows up as the 2-5 m/s wag velocity squared. (vloc.x/vloc.y intentionally unused.)
   785	                    Vector3 floc = new Vector3(
   786	                        0f,
   787	                        0f,
   788	                        -half * sg.areaZ * Mathf.Abs(vloc.z) * vloc.z);
   789	                    Vector3 fworld = R * floc;
   790	                    fDrag += fworld;
   791	                    wDrag += Mathf.Max(0f, -Vector3.Dot(fworld, uj));     // dissipated power (>= 0)
   792	
   793	                    // M_eff += R * diag(maX,maY,maZ) * R^T  (added-mass tensor in world frame)
   794	                    Vector3 cx = R * Vector3.right, cy = R * Vector3.up, cz = R * Vector3.forward;
   795	                    float ax = sg.maX, ay = sg.maY, az = sg.maZ;
   796	                    m00 += ax * cx.x * cx.x + ay * cy.x * cy.x + az * cz.x * cz.x;
   797	                    m01 += ax * cx.x * cx.y + ay * cy.x * cy.y + az * cz.x * cz.y;
   798	                    m02 += ax * cx.x * cx.z + ay * cy.x * cy.z + az * cz.x * cz.z;
   799	                    m11 += ax * cx.y * cx.y + ay * cy.y * cy.y + az * cz.y * cz.y;
   800	                    m12 += ax * cx.y * cx.z + ay * cy.y * cy.z + az * cz.y * cz.z;
   801	                    m22 += ax * cx.z * cx.z + ay * cy.z * cy.z + az * cz.z * cz.z;
   802	                }
   803	
   804	                Vector3 fStream = (tReact + tFin) * _fThrust + fDrag;    // reactive + circulatory foil; NO buoyancy: net vertical zeroed
   805	                Vector3 dv = SolveSym3(m00, m01, m02, m11, m12, m22, fStream * Dt);
   806	                _vCom += dv;
   807	                _vCom.y = 0f;                                            // constrain to the horizontal swim plane
   808	                _xCom += _vCom * Dt;
   809	
   810	                if (measuring)
   811	                {
   812	                    ReactiveImpulse += Mathf.Abs(tReact) * Dt;
   813	                    DragImpulse += Mathf.Abs(Vector3.Dot(fDrag, _fThrust)) * Dt;
   814	                    MechWork += (pWake + pFin + wDrag) * Dt;            // + fin induced/profile wake loss (energy closes: P_in = T*U + pWake)
   815	                    ForwardThrustImpulse += (tReact + tFin) * Dt;        // SIGNED; forward momentum both channels feed the COM (= shed rearward to the wake)
   945	            {
   946	                _t += Dt;
   947	                for (int i = 0; i < _pos.Length; i++) _prevPos[i] = _pos[i];
   948	                Vector3 prevTip = TailTip();               // tail TIP at the PREVIOUS pose (body frame)
   949	                Pose(_t);                                  // body-frame gait, with the guarded +_turnCmd*depth bias (§4.1)
   950	
   951	                // ---- body-frame COM offset, recomputed each step because _pos[] moves with the gait (§3.1) ----
   952	                Vector3 comBF = Vector3.zero;
   953	                for (int j = 0; j < _segs.Count; j++) comBF += _segs[j].mass * _pos[j];
   954	                comBF = _mBody > 1e-6f ? comBF / _mBody : Vector3.zero;
   955	
   956	                Vector3 omegaVec = new Vector3(0f, _omegaY, 0f);   // yaw-only angular velocity (world up)
   957	
   958	                // ---- trailing-edge reactive thrust, sampled from COM + UNDULATORY gait crossflow (NOT rigid ω×r) (§3.4) ----
   959	                Vector3 fwdWorld = _orientation * Vector3.forward;                 // == heading (_fThrust)
   960	                Vector3 latWorld = _orientation * Vector3.right;                   // == lateral (_nThrust)
   961	                Vector3 tipNow = TailTip();                                        // tail TIP at the new pose (body frame)
   962	                Vector3 rTip = _orientation * (tipNow - comBF);                    // world tail-tip moment arm from COM
   963	                Vector3 gaitVel = _orientation * ((tipNow - prevTip) / Dt);        // UNDULATORY tip velocity (gait ∂h/∂t)
   964	                // REACTIVE thrust samples the undulatory crossflow relative to the through-flow -- NOT the rigid whole-
   965	                // body spin (ω×rTip). Rigid rotation is resisted by drag + the yaw form-drag (below), never credited as
   966	                // trailing-edge thrust; feeding ω×rTip here made tReact quadratic in ω -> a self-reinforcing +ω² yaw
   967	                // torque -> clamp-pinned pinwheel (diagnosis 2026-07-02). The Lighthill law is unchanged; its INPUT is.
   968	                Vector3 vReact = _vCom + gaitVel;                                  // (was: _vCom + Cross(omegaVec,rTip) + gaitVel)
   969	                float U = Vector3.Dot(vReact, fwdWorld);
   970	                float Vt = Vector3.Dot(vReact, latWorld);
   971	                float s = Vector3.Dot(_orientation * (_rot[_tail] * Vector3.forward), latWorld);   // tail-backbone slope
   972	                float tReact = ReactiveThrust(_mt, Vt, U, s);
   973	                float Wt = Crossflow(Vt, U, s);                                    // crossflow shed at the tail (work ledger)
   974	                float pWake = WakePower(_mt, Mathf.Max(0f, U), Wt);               // irrecoverable shed-wake power (mirrors Step)
   975	                // Circulatory foil LIFT channel (Task 8; mirror one-shot Step :720-724): tReact already carries the fin's
   976	                // added-mass half via m_t; this adds the bound-circulation lift -> additive physics, Surface caudal fin only.
   977	                float tFin = 0f, pFin = 0f;
   978	                if (_finActive)
   979	                {
   980	                    float vtEff = Vt * Mathf.Lerp(1f, _finAlign, _finPlaneGain);   // Garrick lift ∝ heave·normal; = Vt at gain 0
   981	                    tFin = CirculatoryLiftThrust(_finAL, _finAR, _finS, U, vtEff, s, out pFin);
   982	                }
   983	                Vector3 fThrustW = (tReact + tFin) * fwdWorld;                              // thrust vector, applied at the tail tip
   984	
   985	                // ---- ONE segment loop: world drag (§3.3) + M_eff (existing) + drag torque (§3.5) + I_yaw (§3.6) ----
   986	                Vector3 fDrag = Vector3.zero;
   987	                float wDrag = 0f;                                                  // dissipated axial-drag power (work ledger; mirrors Step)
   988	                float m00 = _mBody * KgPerSimMass, m01 = 0f, m02 = 0f, m11 = _mBody * KgPerSimMass, m12 = 0f, m22 = _mBody * KgPerSimMass;   // structural inertia in SI kg (gene-mass * KgPerSimMass) -> matches the SI added-mass tensor below
   989	                float half = 0.5f * RhoWater * _cd;
   990	                float tauYaw = rTip.x * fThrustW.z - rTip.z * fThrustW.x;         // thrust torque (yaw), about the COM (§3.5)
   991	                float iYaw = 0f;                                                   // structural + added-mass yaw inertia (§3.6)
   992	                for (int j = 0; j < _segs.Count; j++)
   993	                {
   994	                    var sg = _segs[j];
   995	                    Vector3 dPos = _pos[j] - comBF;                                // body-frame arm from COM
   996	                    Vector3 rj = _orientation * dPos;                             // world moment arm (§3.1)
   997	                    Quaternion R = _orientation * _rot[j];                        // world segment rotation (§3.3)
   998	                    Vector3 uj = _vCom + Vector3.Cross(omegaVec, rj)              // rigid COM + rotation ...
   999	                                 + _orientation * ((_pos[j] - _prevPos[j]) / Dt); // ... + gait velocity (rotated to world) (§3.2)
  1000	                    Vector3 vloc = Quaternion.Inverse(R) * uj;                    // segment velocity in local frame
  1001	                    // AXIAL-ONLY form drag on the streamwise local +z (form UNCHANGED from Step; §3.3). The
  1002	                    // lateral broadside terms stay unmodelled -- that momentum is REACTIVE (the EBT m_t below).
  1003	                    Vector3 floc = new Vector3(0f, 0f, -half * sg.areaZ * Mathf.Abs(vloc.z) * vloc.z);
  1004	                    Vector3 fworld = R * floc;
  1005	                    fDrag += fworld;
  1006	                    wDrag += Mathf.Max(0f, -Vector3.Dot(fworld, uj));            // dissipated power (>= 0), mirrors Step
  1007	
  1008	                    // M_eff += R * diag(maX,maY,maZ) * R^T  (world-frame added-mass tensor; EXISTING assembly)
  1009	                    Vector3 cx = R * Vector3.right, cy = R * Vector3.up, cz = R * Vector3.forward;
  1010	                    float ax = sg.maX, ay = sg.maY, az = sg.maZ;
  1011	                    m00 += ax * cx.x * cx.x + ay * cy.x * cy.x + az * cz.x * cz.x;
  1012	                    m01 += ax * cx.x * cx.y + ay * cy.x * cy.y + az * cz.x * cz.y;
  1013	                    m02 += ax * cx.x * cx.z + ay * cy.x * cy.z + az * cz.x * cz.z;
  1014	                    m11 += ax * cx.y * cx.y + ay * cy.y * cy.y + az * cz.y * cz.y;
  1015	                    m12 += ax * cx.y * cx.z + ay * cy.y * cy.z + az * cz.y * cz.z;
  1016	                    m22 += ax * cx.z * cx.z + ay * cy.z * cy.z + az * cz.z * cz.z;
  1017	
  1018	                    // drag torque about the COM (yaw component of r × F) (§3.5)
  1019	                    tauYaw += rj.x * fworld.z - rj.z * fworld.x;
  1020	
  1021	                    // yaw inertia: structural (body-frame == world for a yaw-only orientation, §3.6) + added-mass
  1022	                    // (REUSES the cx/cy/cz columns just built -- the (y,y) reduction of the world added-mass tensor)
  1023	                    iYaw += sg.mass * KgPerSimMass * (dPos.x * dPos.x + dPos.z * dPos.z);   // structural yaw inertia in SI kg -> matches the SI added-mass yaw term below
  1024	                    float ex = cx.x * rj.z - cx.z * rj.x;
  1025	                    float ey = cy.x * rj.z - cy.z * rj.x;
  1026	                    float ez = cz.x * rj.z - cz.z * rj.x;
  1027	                    iYaw += ax * ex * ex + ay * ey * ey + az * ez * ez;
  1028	                }
  1029	
  1030	                // ---- LINEAR (semi-implicit, EXISTING structure): NO vertical terms (§11.4), _vCom.y forced 0 ----
  1031	                Vector3 fStream = fThrustW + fDrag;                               // 2a: no buoyancy/depth (§11.4)
  1032	                Vector3 dv = SolveSym3(m00, m01, m02, m11, m12, m22, fStream * Dt);
  1033	                _vCom += dv;
  1034	                _vCom.y = 0f;                                                     // stay in the horizontal plane (§11.4)
  1035	                _xCom += _vCom * Dt;                                              // COM-DIRECT: _xCom IS the COM (§11.3)
  1036	
  1037	                // ---- ANGULAR MOMENTUM (§11.2): integrate L, derive ω = L/I (NOT ω with a varying I) ----
  1038	                iYaw = Mathf.Max(iYaw, 1e-4f);                                    // epsilon-guard the degenerate body (§3.6)
  1039	                // Real dissipative YAW FORM-DRAG torque (§11.1, Part A): quadratic in ω (echoing the axial v² drag
  1040	                // law), folded into tauYaw BEFORE the momentum update so it is momentum-INTEGRATED. The old post-hoc
  1041	                // `_omegaY *= (1 - AngDampK·Dt)` decay was a BUG under momentum integration: ω is recomputed from
  1042	                // _Lyaw every step, so the decay was discarded next step, _Lyaw grew unbounded under sustained
  1043	                // torque, and ω pegged the MaxOmega clamp -- a covert kinematic fiat. As a REAL torque, τ_thrust
  1044	                // balances Cyaw·ω² at a drag-limited steady ω, so the clamp below never binds in a normal turn.
  1045	                tauYaw += -_Cyaw * _omegaY * Mathf.Abs(_omegaY);
  1046	                _Lyaw += tauYaw * Dt;
  1047	                _omegaY = _Lyaw / iYaw;
  1048	                _omegaY = Mathf.Clamp(_omegaY, -MaxOmega, MaxOmega);             // PURE numerical backstop (§7.2/§11.1); must not bind in a normal turn
  1049	                _orientation = Quaternion.Normalize(
  1050	                    Quaternion.AngleAxis(_omegaY * Dt * Mathf.Rad2Deg, Vector3.up) * _orientation);
  1051	
  1052	                // ---- finite guard, each substep (§6.2): the persistent path has no Sanitize ----
  1053	                if (!IsFinite(_vCom)) _vCom = Vector3.zero;
  1054	                if (!IsFinite(_omegaY)) _omegaY = 0f;
  1055	                if (!IsFinite(_Lyaw)) _Lyaw = 0f;
  1148	        static bool IsFinite(Vector3 v) => IsFinite(v.x) && IsFinite(v.y) && IsFinite(v.z);
  1149	
  1150	        /// Solve M * x = rhs for a symmetric positive-definite 3x3 M = [[m00,m01,m02],[m01,m11,m12],[m02,m12,m22]].
  1151	        static Vector3 SolveSym3(float m00, float m01, float m02, float m11, float m12, float m22, Vector3 rhs)
  1152	        {
  1153	            float c00 = m11 * m22 - m12 * m12;
  1154	            float c01 = m02 * m12 - m01 * m22;
  1155	            float c02 = m01 * m12 - m02 * m11;
  1156	            float det = m00 * c00 + m01 * c01 + m02 * c02;
  1157	            if (Mathf.Abs(det) < 1e-12f) return new Vector3(rhs.x / Mathf.Max(m00, 1e-6f), 0f, rhs.z / Mathf.Max(m22, 1e-6f));
  1158	            float c11 = m00 * m22 - m02 * m02;
  1159	            float c12 = m02 * m01 - m00 * m12;
  1160	            float c22 = m00 * m11 - m01 * m01;
  1161	            float inv = 1f / det;
  1162	            return new Vector3(
  1163	                (c00 * rhs.x + c01 * rhs.y + c02 * rhs.z) * inv,
  1164	                (c01 * rhs.x + c11 * rhs.y + c12 * rhs.z) * inv,
  1165	                (c02 * rhs.x + c12 * rhs.y + c22 * rhs.z) * inv);
  1166	        }
  1167	
  1168	        // -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1169	        // TEST SEAMS (Task 1) -- pure, allocation-light entry points for the EditMode litmus.
  1170	        // -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1198	        /// for `steps` fixed steps, and reports the integrator's streamwise momentum ledger:
  1199	        ///   vFinal               -- COM velocity after the run (started at zero),
  1200	        ///   fHat                 -- the frozen forward heading,
  1201	        ///   forwardThrustImpulse -- Sum(T_react*Dt): the streamwise impulse the trailing-edge thrust fed the COM
  1202	        ///                           (by Newton's third law, the forward momentum shed REARWARD into the wake).
  1203	        /// With Cd=0 the ONLY streamwise force is that reactive thrust, so the integrator obeys, every step,
  1204	        ///   M_eff * dv_com = T_react * fHat * Dt .
  1205	        /// For an ALL-SPHERE body (a=b=c) every segment's added-mass tensor is isotropic AND rotation-invariant,
  1206	        /// so M_eff = (M_body + Sum m_a) * I is CONSTANT and the summed identity collapses to the exact
  1207	        ///   (vFinal . fHat) * (M_body + Sum m_a) == forwardThrustImpulse .
  1208	        /// The L6 test computes (M_body + Sum m_a) INDEPENDENTLY from the reconstructed segments, so a corrupted
  1209	        /// M_eff folding (wrong tensor, lost/extra added mass, wrong sign or normalization) makes vFinal -- and
  1210	        /// thus this identity -- fail. (This validates the FOLDING, unlike a zero-force coast which holds for ANY M_eff.)
  1211	        public static void MomentumLedger(BodyGenome g, int steps, out Vector3 vFinal, out Vector3 fHat, out float forwardThrustImpulse)
  1212	        {
  1213	            var segs = Reconstruct(g, out int tail);
  1214	            Vector3 comRest = CenterOfMass(segs, out float mBody);
  1215	            Vector3 fThrust = Horizontal(comRest - segs[tail].restPos);
  1216	            if (fThrust.sqrMagnitude < 1e-8f) fThrust = Vector3.back;
  1217	            fThrust.Normalize();
  1218	            var sim = new Sim(segs, tail, mBody, g.swimFreq, fThrust, cd: 0f, v0: Vector3.zero, finPlaneGain: 0f);
  1219	            sim.Run(steps, measuring: true);
  1220	            vFinal = sim.Velocity;
  1221	            fHat = fThrust;
  1222	            forwardThrustImpulse = sim.ForwardThrustImpulse;
  1223	        }
  1224	    }
  1225	}
   275	        [Test]
   276	        public void Energy_closes_exactly_P_in_equals_thrust_work_plus_wake()
   277	        {
   278	            // Instantaneous, exact: P_in = T_react*U + P_wake for arbitrary kinematics.
   279	            (float mt, float Vt, float U, float s)[] cases =
   280	            {
   281	                (2f, 0.3f, 0.5f, 0.2f), (1.7f, -0.25f, 1.1f, 0.33f), (0.8f, 0.6f, 0.2f, -0.5f),
   282	            };
   283	            foreach (var (mt, Vt, U, s) in cases)
   284	            {
   285	                float Wt = SwimEval.Crossflow(Vt, U, s);
   286	                float lhs = SwimEval.InputPower(mt, U, Vt, Wt);
   287	                float rhs = SwimEval.ReactiveThrust(mt, Vt, U, s) * U + SwimEval.WakePower(mt, U, Wt);
   288	                Assert.That(lhs, Is.EqualTo(rhs).Within(1e-5f), "P_in = T_react*U + P_wake (term-by-term)");
   289	            }
   290	        }
   291	
   292	        // =====================================================================================
   293	        // TASK 3 -- integrator + episode + Evaluate entry
   294	        // =====================================================================================
   295	
   296	        // A straight tapering axial undulator: tail-ward traveling wave (swimWave>0), increasing
   297	        // amplitude toward the tail. Reaches a finite thrust=drag cruise.
   298	        static BodyGenome Undulator()
   299	        {
   300	            var g = new BodyGenome { swimFreq = 1.5f, swimWave = 1.0f };
   335	            var c2 = Seg(new Vector3(0.25f, 0.2f, 0.45f), 4f, new Vector3(0f, 0f, 0.5f));
   336	            c1.children.Add(c2); rigid.root.children.Add(c1);
   337	            Vector3 v0 = new Vector3(0f, 0f, -0.5f);                 // head-ward, along the body axis
   338	            Vector3 vEnd = SwimEval.CoastTest(rigid, v0, steps: 600); // 5 s
   339	            Assert.That((vEnd - v0).magnitude, Is.LessThan(1e-4f),
   340	                "zero-force coast: the integrator must inject no momentum (sanity; holds for ANY M_eff)");
   341	
   342	            // ---- L6 PROPER: an ACTIVELY undulating all-sphere body with Cd=0. The trailing-edge reactive
   343	            // thrust is the ONLY streamwise force, so each step M_eff*dv = T_react*fHat*Dt. The body's forward
   344	            // momentum gain (folded through the TRUE M_eff) must equal the thrust impulse it was fed -- i.e.
   345	            // the forward momentum shed rearward into the wake. This EXERCISES the added-mass folding.
   346	            var g = SphereUndulator();
   347	            SwimEval.MomentumLedger(g, steps: 600, out Vector3 vFinal, out Vector3 fHat, out float impulse);
   348	
   349	            // Derive the TRUE effective mass INDEPENDENTLY from the reconstructed segments (NOT from the Sim).
   350	            // Every part is a sphere, so M_eff = (M_body + Sum m_a)*I and the streamwise effective mass is
   351	            // M_body + Sum maX. The asserts confirm the spheres really are isotropic (so the closed form is valid).
   352	            var segs = SwimEval.ReconstructForTest(g, out _);
   353	            float mBody = 0f, mAdded = 0f;
   354	            foreach (var sp in segs)
   355	            {
   356	                Assert.That(sp.a, Is.EqualTo(sp.c).Within(1e-6f), "L6 body must be spheres (a==c) for the closed-form M_eff");
   357	                Assert.That(sp.maX, Is.EqualTo(sp.maZ).Within(1e-6f), "sphere added mass must be isotropic (maX==maZ)");
   358	                mBody += sp.mass;
   359	                mAdded += sp.maX;
   360	            }
   361	            float mEff = mBody * 250f + mAdded;   // KgPerSimMass=250: production scales STRUCTURAL mass to SI kg (M_eff SI fix 2026-07-02, SwimEval.cs:643) to match the already-SI added-mass tensor; added mass unscaled
   362	
   363	            // The body actually sheds momentum (guards against a degenerate 0 == 0 pass -- the exact defect
   364	            // the old, force-free L6 had).
   365	            Assert.That(Mathf.Abs(impulse), Is.GreaterThan(1e-3f),
   366	                "active undulation must produce a nonzero thrust impulse (else the ledger is vacuous)");
   367	
   368	            // THE CHECK: forward momentum the body gained, folded through the INDEPENDENT M_eff, must equal the
   369	            // thrust impulse it was fed. vFinal is produced by the PRODUCTION M_eff folding; mEff here is
   370	            // recomputed from the segments. A wrong M_eff (dropped/extra/mis-rotated/mis-signed/mis-normalised

   124	    sirrobin.observe

   550	---
   551	
   552	## 4. S3–S9 — Milestone Contracts
   553	
   554	Lighter by intent: each slice is defined by its *contract* (scope, components, falsifiable done-gate, dependency, top risk). Per-task decomposition is authored when the prior slice's gate goes green (P7). Every slice inherits the standing gates (books close, reproducible-within-seed, no stat vector, no green-keeping dial, telemetry-before-claim). Risk classes: 🟩 engineering · 🟦 engineering-with-frontier-tail · 🟥 research frontier.
   555	
   556	| Slice | Scope | Class | Gating dep | "Done" is falsified if… |
   557	|---|---|---|---|---|
   558	| **S3** | Feeding+metabolism+reproduction on conserved energy | 🟩 | S2 | energy books don't close end-to-end, or population needs a mint/cap knob |
   559	| **S4** | Predation as staged contest between real bodies | 🟦 | S3 | a predatory role never arises unseeded, or prey mass isn't fully accounted |
   560	| **S5** | Speciation / mating / taxonomy | 🟥 | S4 | no disruptive-selection setup produces a durable deterministic split |
   561	| **S6** | Currents / weather / transport | 🟩 | S1 fields | advected fields stop closing books, or no patchy productivity emerges |
   562	| **S7** | Render / observation viewer | 🟩 | S1–S6 telemetry + state contract | render-on vs render-off changes any sim number |
   563	| **S8** | RL / embodiment (Talos) | 🟩 seam / 🟥 gate | S1–S4 green AND Sophia iface verified | a CORE-only policy cannot drive a fish through Step/Reset over the contract |
   564	| **S9** | Plants + bidirectional water↔land | 🟥 | S2 additive core + S3/S4 benthic gradient; full crossing needs all of S1–S6 | sea-robin walk never emerges; then no two-way crossing |
   565	
   566	### S3 — Feeding / Metabolism / Reproduction 🟩
   567	
   568	Close the *energy* loop the way S1 closed the *nutrient* loop.
   569	
   570	**Components:** `graze(pos,C_form,field,cfg,dt)→(I_assim,egesta,field_debit)` Holling-II, `I_bio←min(I_bio,D_local)`, `C_form` from morphology (§6.4); `metabolism(mass,temp,cfg)→P_basal` Kleiber α≈0.79, `P_active=P_basal+P_loco` (SwimEval muscle power); `reproduce(...)` mass-scaled eligibility, paired debit==credit `(E,struct_N)` parent→child, juvenile body from genome (real `M_off`, never a flat tank, §6.5.2); egesta `(1−AE)·I_bio` and death-routed `struct_N`→`Bd`; birth/death into `(W,N_cap)` via free-slot claim / alive-mask clear (deterministic); telemetry: per-cause mortality, lifespan, reserve histograms, `[CONS]` energy line.
   571	
   572	**Acceptance (falsifiable):** in one deterministic run a cohort **feeds→grows→reproduces→dies with energy books closed end-to-end** (`INV-ENERGY < τ_energy`/close, drift bounded-oscillating over ≥1e6 steps); every transfer passes `INV-TRANSFER<1e-6`; population **sustains (plateaus at production÷real-per-capita-intake) without any `carryingCap`/`energyCap` knob** (§6.8–6.9). **Top risk RK-13:** loop oscillates/tuning-fragile → single-dial activation with ledger watched; a collapse is a diagnostic that fidelity was dropped upstream, never fixed by retuning the Joule/SMR anchor.
   573	
   574	### S4 — Predation as a Staged Contest 🟦
   575	
   576	A second trophic level that is *earned*, not seeded: `find→close→seize→consume` in continuous space over the shared spatial hash, reading only form+physics-derived capabilities — never a `carnivory` flag (P8).
   577	
   578	**Components:** `find` two-sided multi-modal detection `Detect(modality)` vs `Signature` across {vision↔light, smell↔chemical, lateral-line↔flow, electro↔electric}, no dominant modality; `close` pursuit from real drag/yaw-torque + burst gear, not a speed stat; `seize` two-sided `GripRate` vs `Evade`, overpower = mass ratio, `F_crack` from jaw geometry; `consume` gated `CanCapture∧CanDigest`, one paired transaction prey `(E,struct_N)`→predator(AE)+detritus(1−AE); spatial-hash neighbor reuse (no cell-bucketed prey mass); telemetry: trophic occupancy, kill/attempt, per-modality stats, mass-flow ledger.
   579	
   580	**Acceptance (falsifiable):** in a run seeded with **no predator**, a predatory strategy **arises implicitly** and transferred mass is fully accounted (prey debit == predator credit + egesta). Report the trophic pyramid + arms-race signature. **Top risk (frontier tail):** emergence depends on encounter economics (world is *dense not large*, §2.9); if no predator emerges, root-cause to encounter economics before any incentive knob (P8 forbids a hunt-reward term).
   581	
   582	### S5 — Speciation / Mating / Taxonomy 🟥
   583	
   584	Genome Phase P2 (§5.12) — the payoff phase, the first hard research-frontier gate.
   585	
   586	**Components:** NEAT compatibility distance `δ` over innovation-aligned genes (batched **within spatial buckets**, not global N²); innovation-aligned crossover (align by iid, matching/disjoint/excess); spatial assortative mating gated `δ<δ_t` within `r_mate`, sweep `(w,r_mate,δ_t)` (bimodal-fragile, RK-9); biological species = connected components of the interbreeding graph; observational taxonomy (phenotypic-cosine Linnaean ladder, one-way latch, procedural naming) — **separate axis, never feeds selection or gates mating**; a disruptive-selection test harness (parameters = open Q #11).
   587	
   588	**Acceptance (falsifiable):** **one deterministic run where a panmictic population splits into two non-interbreeding clusters under disruptive selection**, stable and reproductively isolated for a defined run length. **Do not proceed until demonstrated — or its absence root-caused to the ecology (insufficient niche diversity), not the encoding** (RK-9). Mitigations: magic-trait coupling option; Kleiber + prune pressure so new DOF must earn fitness (RK-10).
   589	
   590	### S6 — Currents / Weather / Transport 🟩
   591	
   592	Coupled velocity/temperature/light fields climbing the circulation ladder behind the unchanged `sample(x)` interface (P4).
   593	
   594	**Components:** **L0** prescribed analytic gyres from stream function `ψ` (divergence-free `u=−∂ψ/∂y, v=∂ψ/∂x`), diel/seasonal solar forcing, hand-placed upwelling — no PDE solve; **flux-form advection** with positivity/flux limiter (INV-W5); **Ekman upwelling** `w_e ∝ curl(τ/ρf)` (a divergence-free horizontal current gives zero upwelling by construction); **L1 (optional)** barotropic QG vorticity on the periodic box with FFT Poisson solve, deterministic on a fixed device; light promoted to a stored cloud/current-coupled field; currents deflect around bathymetry and carry vent plumes.
   595	
   596	**Acceptance (falsifiable):** advected fields **still close the books** (INV-W1/W5, concentrations `≥0`), patchy productivity emerges causally (nutrients upwell, blooms advect downstream, dispersal patterns form), **and every downstream consumer runs unchanged through the field query** (CI check that fields/ecology imports didn't change). **Top risk:** L1 solver stability/determinism → ship L0 first; add L1 only if biology demonstrably needs eddies (P6).
   597	
   598	### S7 — Render / Observation Viewer 🟩
   599	
   600	A thin remote/replay viewer (Unity or web) over the **read-only** `observe` surface; render is a consumer, never a producer.
   601	
   602	**Components:** read-only feed over canonical `(W,N_cap)` tensors + fields (the observation tensor *is* the state, §2.4.3); replay-from-checkpoint from serialized `ColonyState`; viewer client renders morphospace/field heatmaps/trophic structure; CI equivalence harness (same seed headless vs viewer-attached).
   603	
   604	**Acceptance (falsifiable):** **render never feeds fitness** — a deterministic run produces byte-identical (same-device) sim numbers with viewer attached and detached, and the viewer reproduces a checkpointed run from `ColonyState` alone. Any headless-vs-viewer divergence fails the slice. **Top risk:** accidental coupling → the one-way `observe` import boundary + render-off equivalence gate make any leak a CI failure.
   605	
   606	### S8 — RL / Embodiment (Talos) 🟩 seam · 🟥 gate
   607	
   608	Stand up the sim↔Talos state contract: versioned CORE/EXT dict-of-tensors over a gym-shaped Step/Reset env. The seam is day-one architecture; S8 adds schema, serialization, gating.
   609	
   610	**Components:** `Header` + CORE/EXT dict-of-tensors, `(W,N_cap)` batch + alive-mask, SI/REP-103/radians/seconds; **CORE action** = 2-vector `{surge_effort∈[−1,1]→Twist.linear.x, yaw_rate∈[−1,1]→Twist.angular.z}` (validated by StepLive integrating yaw only, §7.4.2); **CORE obs** `lin_vel, ang_vel, heading, range_egocentric(K), flow_rel, energy, contact`; **EXT** (sim-only) chemical gradients (the fish's *primary* sense, deliberately EXT — no robot analogue), light/depth/temp, proprioception, feeding strike; serialization ladder in-process torch-dict → Protobuf proto3 → ROS2 `.msg`/`geometry_msgs/Twist`; conformance test freezing a CORE fixture so **pydantic + Gymnasium space + `.msg` agree field-for-field**; Talos-side symbolic→continuous adapter kept **out of** the sim schema.
   611	
   612	**Acceptance (falsifiable):** a headless gym-style `Step`/`Reset` env drives a sim fish end-to-end — a **CORE-only** policy produces coherent locomotion through the versioned schema, conformance fixture passes across pydantic/Gymnasium/`.msg`. **Dual hard gate (§7.4.4):** (1) books closed — S1–S4 invariants green; (2) **the Sophia symbolic-vs-continuous action interface verified against live Sophia code**, not the `ExecutorShim` placeholder. **Top risk RK-4** (highest strategic): contract continuous-first; symbolic decode lives Talos-side; if Sophia is continuous the adapter collapses to pass-through. Secondary open-Qs #8 (ROS2 distro), #9 (in-process vs out-of-process).
   613	
   614	### S9 — Plants + Bidirectional Water↔Land 🟥
   615	
   616	The emblematic endpoint, staged: **(9a) sea-robin milestone** — a benthic fish walking on re-purposed fin-rays *inside the water* (de-risks the frontier); **(9b) full bidirectional crossing** — sea→land limb and land→sea re-streamlining on the additive medium-dependent physics with **no mode switch**. Also lands plants (L-system/CPPN, genome P3) and the land/rivers/sediment cascade (§3.7).
   617	
   618	**Components:** additive `Gravity`, `Buoyancy` (ellipsoid displaced volume), `Contact/Friction` contributors on the already-additive core (S2, no rewrite); continuous `medium(x)` submersion-fraction query (§3.11, a smooth gradient, not a boolean); land cascade `is_land = elevation > sea_level`, flow-accumulation rivers→`river_sources`, sediment/burial as a **transfer** to a tracked reservoir (never deletion), intertidal two-way corridor with a survivable ramp both directions; plant kingdom rooted in substrate; genome reversibility guard (`Segment↔Surface` flip + neutral drift lets a walking limb drift back to swimming — no irreversible ratchet); benthic-foraging gradient (buried prey reachable by ventral fin-ray probing).
   619	
   620	**Acceptance (falsifiable) — staged.**
   621	- **9a (milestone):** ≥1 established lineage where (1) ventral Surface segments spend a measured gait fraction in seafloor contact producing **net upward + forward ground-reaction impulse**; (2) the same appendages **retain nonzero swimming thrust**; (3) the walking capability is **heritable and improves under the benthic gradient**. Report the impulse budget + gait phase from telemetry (§5.10, §4.6).
   622	- **9b (frontier):** an **emergent, unscripted crossing of the water/land interface in both directions** on the additive physics with no mode switch; convergent re-derivation of streamlined aquatic form by independent lineages as the signature that physics drives form (§1.6).
   623	
   624	**Top risk (research frontier):** the crossing may not occur — capability is necessary but not sufficient; the *gradient* (survivable ramp, unexploited niche) is the open variable. Secondary: an irreversible ratchet in a mutation operator would break the bidirectional requirement — guarded by the reversibility test.
   625	
   626	### Engineering vs research-frontier at a glance
   627	
   628	| Slice | Mechanism (build) | Emergent outcome (bet) |
   629	|---|---|---|
   630	| S3 | 🟩 solved | population persistence — 🟩 |
   631	| S4 | 🟩 solved | unseeded predator + arms race — 🟦 |
   632	| S5 | 🟩 solved | **deterministic species split — 🟥 (RK-9)** |
   633	| S6 | 🟩 solved | patchy productivity/dispersal — 🟩 |
   634	| S7 | 🟩 solved | — |
   635	| S8 | 🟩 solved seam | **gated on external Sophia verification — 🟥 (RK-4)** |
   636	| S9 | 🟩 solved | **sea-robin walk — 🟥 near-term; two-way crossing — 🟥 long-horizon** |
   637	
   638	---
   639	
   640	## 5. Verification, Salvage & Language Protocol
   640	## 5. Verification, Salvage & Language Protocol
   641	
   642	### 5.1 Why conservation replaces byte-identity (the load-bearing inversion)
   643	
   644	The donor's gate was a seeded-run FNV/BitConverter hash vs a stored golden — satisfiable by code that does nothing, which is *precisely* how faithful mechanics shipped disabled behind `gain=0`/`couple=0` dials to keep goldens green (§1.2, §7.1.1). **Byte-identity answers "did the numbers change?"; conservation answers "do the books close?"** and exerts zero pressure to gate mechanics off. Byte-identity survives only as a same-device regression tripwire (§5.4), never the pass/fail gate.
   645	
   646	### 5.2 Conservation gate as concrete assertions (PRIMARY, P1)
   647	
   648	Every tracked `Q ∈ {nutrient_mass [mol], energy [J]}` lives in a fixed named-reservoir registry; the ledger is f64 compensated. Every mutation goes through `transfer`/`declare_external` — no raw `R_i += x`.
   649	
   650	| Assertion | Threshold | Dtype | Source |
   651	|---|---|---|---|
   652	| Per-step inventory residual | `< 1e-9` | f64 ledger | §7.1.1, S1 AC |
   653	| 1e6-step drift, bounded-oscillating (slope≈0 in noise, `max<1e-6`) | `< 1e-6` | f64 | §7.1.1 |
   654	| `test_no_free_channel`: `|ΣΔR_i − X_ext_step| < τ_cons` | catches free-channel | — | CI-CONS-1 |
   655	| Instantaneous power identity | `< 1e-6` | f32 | §4.8 |
   656	| 1e5-step KE budget | `< 1e-3` f32 / `< 1e-6` f64 | both | §4.8 |
   657	| Metabolic: `ΔE=ΔW_mech/(η·N)`, η=0.20, N=300 J — no path banks at η=1 | identity | — | §4.8 |
   658	
   659	Gate on the drift *curve*, never the endpoint — a slow monotone leak is the failure mode (RK-13).
   660	
   661	### 5.3 Determinism plan
   662	
   663	Target = bit-identical across two same-machine, same-`(device,dtype,op-order)` reruns with a fixed seed. Cross-machine / CPU↔GPU bit-identity is explicitly out of scope; the conservation gate carries cross-device *correctness*. `seed_everything(seed)`: seeds torch/numpy/python, `use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`, `CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG=:4096:8`, no cudnn benchmark. **No nondeterministic atomic scatter** — all births (unique free-slot), segment reductions (`body_id` via `segment_index_add`), development scatter use precomputed unique indices; a Grep CI check forbids raw `scatter_add_`/non-unique `index_add_` in the hot loop. `test_within_seed_rerun`: `max_abs(Δ)==0` on the fixed device, gated only in deterministic mode; GPU-nondeterministic drift reported informationally. RNG manifest: adding an inert gene leaves the seeded stream byte-identical. **Open Q #4: recommend "bit-identical per-device"** (free on CPU, the cheaper contract to defend) over "within 1e-5 rel" (which would let nondeterministic GPU scatter dodge RK-6).
   664	
   665	### 5.4 Bit-golden as a demoted tripwire
   666	
   667	A same-device-CPU golden hash regression, run **nightly, reported not gated**. Answers "did anything change unexpectedly?" as a review aid; can never block a merge; no mechanic may ship dark to keep it green. The only surviving role of the donor's byte-identity harness.
   668	
   669	### 5.5 Test culture
   670	
   671	Tolerance/invariant tests, never golden-value tests (`closure_residual < τ`, `|ported−oracle|/|oracle| < τ_rel`, drift bounded-oscillating — never `state_hash == 0xDEADBEEF`). Long-horizon trajectory divergence under the chaotic gait is *expected* and must not be gated (F5). Fakes over mocks; boundaries over internals — every subsystem tested through its published contract (`FakeGeology`, `FakeField`, `FakeBody`). `test_import_boundary.py` runs the import-linter contract as a pytest and asserts interface opacity (INV-W4). Telemetry-first: every foundation slice ships a headless observation surface before any render; **no claim of correctness or performance is ever made from code inspection — only from a telemetry artifact.**
   672	
   673	### 5.6 Oracle-fixture regression vs the C# donor
   674	
   675	The donor is **never called live** — compiled once into `oracle/SirRobinOracle.csproj` (Unity-light, driven by `Reconstruct`/`LambK`/`Coast`/`MomentumLedger` + `StepTrace`) emitting frozen fixtures committed to the repo (§2.4 table). **Gate on aggregates + short-horizon forces, NOT a long-horizon bit-trace** (chaotic gait, F5) — this is what lets the port be *correct* without being *bit-identical to C#* across an episode.
   676	
   677	> **Migration hazard (RK-11, open Q #3):** the donor stored box mass with ellipsoid `π/6` displacement. The port uses **ellipsoid volume for both**, shifting the added-mass baseline — validate against the box baseline first (Stage A/gain0), then re-record fixtures against the ellipsoid reinterpretation (Stage B/gain1, S0/T11 and S2/P1). Do **not** silently re-baseline. Porting validation rule (uniform): no port is done until it passes both its donor tolerance golden **and** its conservation invariant — a balanced sign error passes energy closure but fails the force-term fixture (RK-7).
   678	
   679	### 5.7 Salvage disposition of every donor module
   680	

codex
The plan should not be approved. Its S0 authorization gate is built on invalid physics accounting, an unrealizable deterministic reduction primitive, and contradictory tensor-shape requirements. The build will fail in S0 before throughput numbers mean anything.

1. **The S0 energy gate is mathematically wrong.**

   The instantaneous assertion omits circulatory thrust work:

   `p_in = tReact·U + pWake + tFin·U + pFin`

   but the plan checks only `tReact·U + pWake + pFin` ([plan:345](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:345)). Any active fin either fails the gate or forces `p_in` to be defined inconsistently. The donor explicitly defines fin input as `T·U + pWake` ([SwimEval.cs:389](</mnt/c/Users/cddal/game prototype/Assets/ProceduralWorld/Scripts/Life/SwimEval.cs:389>)).

   The 100k-step KE test is worse. `M_eff` changes with articulated pose, so kinetic energy is `½vᵀM(t)v`; its change contains a `½vᵀΔM v` term. The proposed comparison against `Σ(P_musc−P_diss)dt` omits that term, discrete semi-implicit work, constraint impulse, and the difference between tail-tip `U` and COM velocity. The donor only proves an algebraic trailing-edge flux identity, not whole-body KE conservation ([SwimEval.cs:320](</mnt/c/Users/cddal/game prototype/Assets/ProceduralWorld/Scripts/Life/SwimEval.cs:320>)).

   **Fix:** Separate reactive and fin algebraic tests, then derive a discrete balance for the actual integrator including `M_n→M_{n+1}`, actuator work, constraint work, drag, and wake losses. Do not use the current KE gate for authorization.

2. **The horizontal dynamics solve is physically invalid.**

   The kernel solves the full 3×3 system, then sets `v_y=0` ([plan:308-309](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:308)). When rotated anisotropic added mass creates `m01` or `m12`, the unconstrained vertical DOF changes the computed x/z acceleration. Deleting y afterward destroys momentum and energy. The donor contains the same bug ([SwimEval.cs:804](</mnt/c/Users/cddal/game prototype/Assets/ProceduralWorld/Scripts/Life/SwimEval.cs:804>)); matching the oracle merely reproduces it.

   **Fix:** Solve the constrained x/z system, or solve a KKT system with an explicit vertical reaction impulse. Account for that impulse in the ledger and deliberately re-baseline the donor.

3. **`segment_index_add` cannot satisfy the determinism claim as specified.**

   `body_id` necessarily contains duplicate indices—several segments reduce into each body. “Precomputed unique slots” cannot make those destination indices unique without first performing a separate deterministic reduction. The plan names no algorithm for that reduction while simultaneously forbidding the standard atomic implementations ([plan:213-223](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:213), [plan:663](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:663)).

   A grep rule is not an algorithm, and hiding `index_add_` inside the sanctioned wrapper does not make it deterministic.

   **Fix:** Specify and prototype the actual CUDA reduction now: fixed padded-tree reduction, sorted CSR plus deterministic prefix reduction, or a custom fixed-order kernel. Gate bit identity on that exact implementation before building the rest of S0.

4. **The “static-shape” architecture contradicts the flattened live-segment layout.**

   Creature tensors are fixed-capacity, but segment tensors have shape `[S_total]`, where `S_total` is the sum of live segment counts ([plan:165-178](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:165), [plan:287-293](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:287)). Birth, death, refill, mutation, or compaction changes `S_total`. That invalidates CUDA-graph capture, changes allocation addresses, recompiles dynamic graphs, and makes `body_ptr` maintenance nontrivial.

   The design’s later development representation is actually fixed `[P,S_max,...]` ([design:638-652](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/2026-07-11-sirrobin-design-document.md:638)), contradicting the plan’s insistence that padded storage is only an ablation.

   **Fix:** Choose one real representation. Either use fixed `[B,S_max]` storage, or define a fixed-capacity segment arena with allocation, fragmentation, generations, compaction, pointer repair, and graph-recapture policies. Benchmark that real lifecycle.

5. **The f32 transfer ledger cannot close exactly “by construction.”**

   Debiting and crediting the same f32 amount does not preserve the f64-observed total when source and destination have different magnitudes. For example, subtracting `0.5` from `1.0` is represented, while adding it to `1e8` rounds away. A compensated f64 sum afterward cannot recover information already lost in the f32 writes.

   Therefore `residual==0`, `<1e-9`, and million-step `<1e-6` are unsupported for f32 reservoirs ([plan:414-425](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:414), [plan:459-471](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:459)).

   **Fix:** Use f64 reservoir state, fixed-point/integer quanta, or track every realized post-rounding debit/credit discrepancy in an explicit numerical-residual reservoir. Set tolerances from measured scale-dependent error bounds.

6. **The throughput gate is arbitrary, internally contradictory, and not end-to-end.**

   Near-term scope is one world with hundreds to low thousands of creatures, but the floor is derived from the deferred S8 target of 256 worlds, then multiplied by another unexplained 10× ([design:268-272](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/2026-07-11-sirrobin-design-document.md:268), [plan:350](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:350)). That effectively demands about `3.07e8` creature-steps/s without deriving it from a required experiment duration.

   Worse, S0 measures only frozen-heading locomotion. It excludes development, fields, spatial hashing, feeding, encounters, mutation, mating, telemetry, and the real StepLive kernel. Passing it cannot authorize “the entire vectorization thesis.”

   **Fix:** Define required wall-clock time for a scientifically useful run, convert it to end-to-end simulated-years/hour, and benchmark a representative whole tick. Keep kernel throughput as diagnostic telemetry, not architectural authorization.

7. **S1’s acceptance behavior is impossible with the processes S1 implements.**

   S1 claims a bloom must grow, draw down nutrient, and crash, and that drifters plateau at production divided by intake ([plan:469-475](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:469)). But S1 contains no `Bp→Bd` mortality/respiration process and no grazing; feeding is deferred to S3. Production therefore transfers `Nd→Bp` until nutrient exhaustion, after which `Bp` simply remains. No crash and no consumer plateau can occur.

   **Fix:** Add explicit producer loss, respiration, mortality, and `Bp→Bd/Nd` transfers to S1, or remove those acceptance claims and defer them to S3.

8. **The gain1 oracle is circular and does not exist in the live donor.**

   The live donor hard-codes `ellipMassGain = 0` ([SwimEval.cs:83](</mnt/c/Users/cddal/game prototype/Assets/ProceduralWorld/Scripts/Life/SwimEval.cs:83)); `ReconstructForTest` has no gain parameter. `StepTraceForTest` also does not exist. The plan proposes modifying or reimplementing the oracle and then “re-recording” gain1 fixtures ([plan:324-337](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:324), [plan:386](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:386)).

   Once both the port and oracle are changed to the new model, agreement is not independent validation; the same misunderstanding can exist in both.

   **Fix:** Freeze an untouched donor executable for gain0. For gain1, derive independent analytic fixtures for mass, added mass, force, and momentum, or add a narrowly reviewed parameter seam while retaining provenance and validating it against gain0 byte-for-byte.

9. **The pose port has invalid root and empty-body indexing.**

   Roots have `seg_parent_g=-1`, but the stated depth pass gathers every parent before updating depth-selected rows ([plan:291-303](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:291)). In PyTorch, `-1` gathers the final segment; it does not mean identity. Empty bodies similarly cannot safely execute `pos[tail_gidx]`. `torch.where` does not prevent those gathers from being evaluated.

   **Fix:** Allocate explicit identity/sentinel rows and remap every root/empty parent and tail index to them before any gather. Test empty, root-only, maximum-depth, and mixed live/dead batches.

10. **The quaternion inverse is not the donor operation.**

   The plan computes local velocity using `quat_rotate(conj(rot), uj)` ([plan:307](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:307)). The donor uses `Quaternion.Inverse(R)` ([SwimEval.cs:1000](</mnt/c/Users/cddal/game prototype/Assets/ProceduralWorld/Scripts/Life/SwimEval.cs:1000)). Conjugate equals inverse only for an exactly unit quaternion. Repeated float32 compositions do not guarantee exact unit norm.

   **Fix:** Implement `q⁻¹=conj(q)/‖q‖²` with the donor’s degenerate semantics, or prove and enforce normalization at every matching donor point. Oracle-test long pose chains, not just isolated rotations.

11. **The float32/float64 policy will accidentally push the hot loop into float64.**

   The plan says LambK produces `seg_ma` in f64 and that `StepLedger`, `dv`, and `m_eff` are f64-like diagnostic data ([plan:312-314](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:312)), while also claiming a float32 hot loop and warning about consumer-GPU FP64 throughput. Mixing f64 added mass with f32 rotations promotes the M_eff assembly and solve.

   Per-body f64 ledgers written every step are also not a cheap “global compensated ledger.”

   **Fix:** Compute LambK in f64 once, explicitly cast stored hot-loop coefficients to f32, and keep high-precision validation in a separate test configuration. State exactly which tensors participate in the throughput gate.

12. **`SolveSym3` is treated as a trivial port despite being numerically fragile.**

   The donor’s absolute `|det|<1e-12` branch is not scale-invariant ([SwimEval.cs:1151](</mnt/c/Users/cddal/game prototype/Assets/ProceduralWorld/Scripts/Life/SwimEval.cs:1151)). Cofactor inversion can produce catastrophic relative error for ill-conditioned matrices long before that threshold fires. Comparing ordinary random cases against NumPy does not cover the actual failure region ([plan:375-383](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:375)).

   Compilation may also fuse or reassociate expressions, defeating the claimed “exact donor op-order.”

   **Fix:** Generate M_eff matrices from extreme legal morphologies, test residual `‖Mx−b‖`, condition number, finiteness, and compile/eager parity. Add scale-relative regularization or a robust SPD solve for production, retaining the cofactor form only for donor conformance.

13. **The RNG manifest does not guarantee stream stability.**

   A zero-count entry for an inert gene preserves downstream draws only while it remains inert. Once activated and consuming draws, every subsequent sequential draw shifts. Slot recycling and compaction further change iteration order. There is no stable entity ID in `ColonyState`, so RNG cannot even be keyed to organism identity ([plan:167-180](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:167), [plan:663](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:663)).

   **Fix:** Use counter-based keyed RNG streams such as `(seed, step, stable_entity_id, gene_iid, event, draw_index)`. Add stable entity IDs, parent IDs, allocator state, and innovation registry state to checkpoints.

14. **“Replay from ColonyState alone” is false.**

   The listed `ColonyState` omits field reservoirs, genomes, developed bodies or their regeneration inputs, RNG states, stable IDs, free-list state, innovation registry, configuration, clock schedule, and external forcing state. Yet S7 requires replay from that object alone ([plan:602-604](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:602)).

   **Fix:** Define a versioned `SimulationSnapshot` containing all authoritative state. `ColonyState` can remain the creature-state subset, but it cannot be advertised as a complete checkpoint.

15. **The ecological energy ledger has no actual closed system.**

   S3 debits basal metabolism and locomotion but identifies no heat/waste-energy reservoir. Primary production introduces chemical energy from light, yet S1 models light as an untracked analytic value. Predation and feeding also mix nutrient mass and energy without a defined biomass energy-density conversion. “Energy books closed end-to-end” is therefore rhetoric, not an implementable equation ([plan:566-572](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:566), [plan:646-657](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:646)).

   **Fix:** Define every energy reservoir and external flux: incident/absorbed light, chemical biomass, reserve, mechanical work, wake/drag dissipation, metabolic heat, and exported energy. Specify unit conversions and transaction equations before S1/S3 implementation.

16. **The milestone dependency graph contradicts itself.**

   The milestone table says S5 depends on S4 ([plan:556-564](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:556)). The critical-path text says `S3→S5` while S4 branches separately ([plan:742](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:742)). S6 nominally depends only on S1, yet the same paragraph postpones it until S5. These are mutually incompatible build sequences.

   **Fix:** Publish one DAG with explicit artifact-level dependencies and remove the prose alternatives.

17. **S4 demands evolution before an evolutionary engine is specified.**

   S4 requires an unseeded predator to arise, but the plan does not specify the mutation/selection loop, mutation rates, inheritance mechanics, or sufficient sensory fields before that gate. It simultaneously asks for vision, smell, lateral line, and electroreception despite several associated environmental fields not existing yet ([plan:574-580](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:574)).

   **Fix:** Land and validate asexual mutation, inheritance, stable lineage tracking, and the minimum required sensing environment before making emergence an S4 gate. Seed a functional predator for mechanism validation; reserve “unseeded emergence” for a separate research experiment.

18. **The import-linter contract does not enforce “contracts-only crossings.”**

   The forbidden contract covers only two source modules and four specific destination modules ([plan:114-140](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:114)). It does not prevent `core` from importing concrete internals, `observe` from reaching everything, or consumers from importing most non-contract modules. The claimed firewall is mostly absent.

   **Fix:** Define exhaustive forbidden/independence contracts for every layer and explicitly permit only public contract modules.

19. **The benchmark program is not executable as a one-week task.**

   The advertised sweep has 576 cells before repetitions, compilation, graph capture, profiler runs, correctness tests, 100k-step energy runs, fixture generation, and two layouts. `K`, timing duration, memory limits, timeout policy, compile-cache isolation, graph-recapture rules, and which work is included are unspecified ([plan:367-369](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:367), [plan:821-825](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-implementation-plan.md:821)).

   Profiling every cell will itself dominate the schedule and compiled graphs will obscure the requested operator attribution.

   **Fix:** Use a staged benchmark design: correctness corpus, small crossover sweep, selected representative H1/H2 cells, then profiler only on the winning and failing configurations. Budget GPU-hours and define statistical confidence.

20. **The risk register badly understates where failure will occur.**

   It labels the gain1 mass migration low/medium, deterministic reduction medium, S1 closure “solved,” and S3/S4 mechanisms solved. Those are the load-bearing unresolved problems. The likely first failure sequence is:

   1. deterministic ragged reduction has no implementation;
   2. flattened churn breaks static capture;
   3. the energy gate fails because its equation is wrong;
   4. gain1 oracle generation requires altering the oracle;
   5. S1 cannot produce its required bloom crash;
   6. end-to-end throughput bears no relation to the S0 number.

   Until those are corrected, S0 is not a go/no-go spike. It is an expensive benchmark of a partially ported donor bug against self-authored fixtures.
